What stays open under §6: the rule reaches all three sections of a relayed answer: the answer section is filtered like the authority and the additional section, and a record whose owner name is not in the domain of the query is dropped, with one exception the DNS itself demands — the records the query's own alias chain leads to (the target of a CNAME at the query name, and the records at that name) ride with the answer, because they are the answer the query asked for and a CNAME chain that leaves the domain of the query is a correct answer; a record that belongs to no chain of the query, the address of a mail exchanger in another domain for instance, is dropped in whichever section it arrives, which is the data RFC 5452 section 6 names as the example of what must not be accepted uncritically, and there is no iterative recursion, so no NS or DS referral is followed. §9.2's unpredictable-source-port MUST is met on this path — every attempt leaves from an OS-assigned ephemeral port drawn from the largest practicable available range, and a retry never reuses its predecessor's port — while its multiple-simultaneous-ports MUST is not exercised, because the UDP ingress loop answers one client query at a time; the per-attempt socket is what keeps any future concurrent queries on distinct ports. §9.2's means to avoid usage of certain ports now exists: the `forwarder-deny-port` directive names a port, or a port range as `first-last`, that the forwarder must not use as a source port, and every outgoing query binds a source port outside that set — a socket bound to a denied port is closed before it carries a query, another one is bound where the resolver owns the source port, and the query fails rather than leave from a denied port. Without the directive every ephemeral port is allowed, which is the behavior before it. A denied port the forwarder would not use anyway, such as 53 when the forwarder is not the local resolver, changes nothing. The other §9.2 SHOULD-level items are still absent: a multi-homed host cannot choose among its source addresses unpredictably, because the socket binds the unspecified address and lets the routing table pick; and no TSIG, SIG(0), or IPsec channel authenticates the hop to the forwarder. | `partial`: the exact forwarder matching subset above, plus §6's in-domain rule for the additional and the authority section of a relayed upstream answer, and the referral rule that keeps only the glue of the delegation the query is about, so a referral for another domain relays none. The row cannot become `supported`: §6's rule reaches the answer, the authority, and the additional section of a relayed upstream answer, with the records the query's alias chain leads to riding with the answer, and the §9.2 items that need a hop the forwarder cannot choose for itself — the unpredictable source address and the authenticated channel — stay out of scope until iterative recursion exists. | [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig) | Upstream matching and classification unit tests in [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig) ("upstream packet classification isolates validation and truncation", "resolver rejects ECS-bearing upstream responses before cache"), plus the socket-level tests "resolver accepts only an exact UDP upstream response" and "upstream UDP attempts each bind a fresh ephemeral source port" in [`test/integration/pipeline_test.zig`](../test/integration/pipeline_test.zig); the latter leaves the first attempt unanswered, watches the loopback forwarder record the source port of both attempts, and fails if the retry reused the first port. Twelve unit tests in [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig) cover the in-domain filter ("RFC 5452 section 6: an answer record outside the domain of the query is not relayed", "RFC 5452 section 6: the records a CNAME chain leads to are relayed", "RFC 5452 section 6: an additional record outside the domain of the query is not relayed", "RFC 5452 section 6: the cached answer holds the filtered additional section", "RFC 5452 section 6: a referral keeps only the glue of the delegation it names", "RFC 5452 section 6: a referral whose glue is out of the zone keeps none of it", "RFC 5452 section 6: a referral for another domain keeps none of its glue", "RFC 5452 section 6: a name in the domain of the query is the query name, an ancestor, or a descendant", "RFC 5452 section 6: an authority record outside the domain of the query is not relayed", "RFC 5452 section 6: a negative answer keeps its SOA", "RFC 5452 section 6: the cached answer holds the filtered authority section", and "RFC 5452 section 6: a referral keeps the NS records of its delegation"); the first two forward to a fake upstream whose answer carries an additional record for an unrelated name beside records for the query name and for a name below it, and they fail when the filter is removed; the third, the fourth and the fifth forward to a fake upstream that answers with a referral whose additional section carries the glue of the delegation beside a record for the query name and one for an unrelated name, the third fails when the glue of the delegation is dropped, the fourth fails when the filter is removed, and the fifth fails when the glue of a delegation the query is not about is relayed; the seventh and the eighth forward to a fake upstream whose answer carries an authority record for an unrelated name beside one for the query name, and whose negative answer carries the zone SOA of a zone of its own beside a record for an unrelated name, the seventh fails when the filter is removed and the eighth fails when the SOA of a negative answer is dropped; the ninth repeats the seventh from the cache, so it fails when the cached copy holds the unfiltered authority section; the tenth forwards to a fake upstream that answers with a referral for a zone the query is not about, and it fails when the NS records of the delegation are dropped. The denied source port is covered by two unit tests in [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig): "RFC 5452 section 9.2: the upstream socket never binds a denied source port" denies every port the operating system can assign and asserts that no query leaves the forwarder at all, and it fails when the denylist is ignored; "RFC 5452 section 9.2: a denied port the forwarder would not use anyway changes nothing" denies port 53 and asserts that the query is still forwarded. |
| CLI surface (commands, flags, refusals) | [RFC 1035 §4.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc1035#section-4.1.1): the header, question, and RR wire format the client builds, validates, and renders; [RFC 3597 §5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3597#section-5): generic `TYPE####` presentation for types with no typed renderer; [RFC 9499](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9499): current DNS terminology (it obsoletes RFC 8499) for the names the CLI accepts. No RFC defines a DNS resolver command line; this row exists so the operator surface is held to the same evidence rule as every protocol row, and it is also where the configuration-file surface is recorded because the matrix has no separate configuration row. | `supported`: `--help`, `-h`, `help`, `--version`, `server run`, and `query` are implemented with deterministic output and fail-closed refusals — an unknown command, an unknown server option, a missing flag value, and an unsupported query type each print an error and exit 2 — with the help and refusal exit paths covered by the documented-invocation guard (`test/cli/documented_invocation_test.zig`, every documented argv executed against the built binary, unclassifiable outcomes fail); `--version` reports the `-Dversion` build option (default `0.1.0`) from the single source in [`build.zig`](../build.zig) with only its rendering unit-tested, and there is no JSON output, dig-style flag set, zone/reload subcommand, or administration surface. **A `listen` directive supplies the address a mode binds; it does not select the mode.** `findListenerAddress` ([`src/main.zig`](../src/main.zig):571) maps the already-chosen mode to a transport and returns the address of the first `listen` entry with that transport, and returns null for `.none`, so a configuration whose only server directives are `listen` lines starts nothing: `zimdns server run --config examples/config.gdns` prints `error: server run currently requires --once-udp, --once-tcp, --serve-udp, --serve-tcp, --serve-dot, --serve-doh, or --serve-xfr` and exits 2. `examples/config.gdns` carries a comment saying so, because that file is where the misreading starts. | `supported`: the same command and flag set, with the documented-invocation guard in place. Deriving the mode from the `listen` directives is deliberately not implemented: the mode flag stays the single explicit choice, and the example configuration states the relationship instead. | [`src/main.zig`](../src/main.zig), [`src/cli/query.zig`](../src/cli/query.zig), [`src/config/parser.zig`](../src/config/parser.zig), [`examples/config.gdns`](../examples/config.gdns) | 26 tests in [`src/main.zig`](../src/main.zig) — two listener-address tests ("no-config listener default is loopback", "configured loop modes use their transport listener endpoint"), the nineteen "query parser accepts/rejects …" type-vocabulary tests, "query command renders a maximum escaped URI target", and "--version prints the build version, not a placeholder" — plus 37 tests in [`src/cli/query.zig`](../src/cli/query.zig) ("query builder encodes standard A query", "response validation requires QR and the exact question", "query formatter prints A and CNAME answers", "query formatter prints DNSSEC RRs in RFC 3597 generic form", "query formatter prints MX SOA PTR DNAME and typed HINFO"); socket-level CLI coverage in [`test/integration/pipeline_test.zig`](../test/integration/pipeline_test.zig) ("CLI TCP exchange uses the bounded connection path"); config-file surface in [`test/config/config_test.zig`](../test/config/config_test.zig). The transport-to-address mapping is covered by "configured loop modes use their transport listener endpoint"; the config-alone refusal is not covered by an automated test, because it exits through `std.process.exit`. |
| DNS name case handling | [RFC 1035 §2.3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc1035#section-2.3.3): case-insensitive comparison of names, with the original case preserved whenever possible; [RFC 1035 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc1035#section-3.1): name servers and resolvers must compare labels case-insensitively; [RFC 4343 §4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4343#section-4.1) and [§4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4343#section-4.2): case preservation on output and on input, clarifying and updating RFC 2181. RFC 2181's own name-server clarifications are tracked in the "Name-server clarifications (RFC 2181)" row below, so this row holds only the case rules. 0x20 QNAME randomization is specified only by the expired Internet-Draft [draft-vixie-dnsext-dns0x20](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-vixie-dnsext-dns0x20), not by any RFC. | `supported` for the RFC rules this row names: name comparison is case-insensitive, and the question's name keeps the case the client put on the wire (`Name.parsePreservingCase` in [`src/dns/name.zig`](../src/dns/name.zig)), so the response question echoes the received case and the forwarder rebuilds its upstream query with that case instead of a folded one. The `query` client now randomizes the case of the first letter of every label of the QNAME it sends, and only when the operator asks for it with `--0x20`: the case is drawn from the system CSPRNG, the rest of every label keeps its case, and the name still compares equal (RFC 1035 section 2.3.3), so the server's comparison still matches the name while a forged answer has to carry the exact case to be accepted. The client prints the question the server echoed, so the case that went on the wire is visible. A zone's own stored case reaches the wire: the owner name of a served record and the names inside its RDATA — the CNAME, NS, DNAME, PTR, MX, RT, AFSDB, RP and SRV targets, the NAPTR replacement and the SOA's two names — keep the case the zone file wrote them in, because RFC 4343 section 4.1 does no case conversion on output: "No 'case conversion' or 'case folding' is done during such output operations, thus 'preserving' case". The signature and the digest keep the canonical form: the RRSIG the signer makes is over the RFC 4034 section 6.2 canonical RR form, in which every name is lowercase, and the ZONEMD digest covers that same form, so a zone written in upper case and the same zone written in lower case digest the same and the project's own validator accepts the answer the server serves. The NSEC3 hash folds the name it is given (RFC 5155 section 5), and the trust-anchor suffix match and the local-zone table compare without regard to case (RFC 1035 section 2.3.3). The server cannot randomize a name on the client's behalf, because the mechanism needs the client's own random source, and the 0x20 mechanism is specified only by the expired Internet-Draft, so the server-side half is a capability the profile does not claim. | `supported` for the RFC rules this row names: the case-insensitive comparison, the preserved question case, the stored case on the wire, and the opt-in client-side 0x20 randomization of the label-first letters; the draft encodes one random bit per ASCII letter, while this profile randomizes the first letter of every label. The authoritative answers and the AXFR and IXFR answers all carry the owner name the zone file spelled. The client-side randomization follows an expired Internet-Draft, so it is no RFC requirement and stays off unless the operator asks for it. | [`src/dns/name.zig`](../src/dns/name.zig), [`src/dns/question.zig`](../src/dns/question.zig), [`src/dns/message.zig`](../src/dns/message.zig), [`src/dns/dnssec.zig`](../src/dns/dnssec.zig), [`src/dns/validate.zig`](../src/dns/validate.zig), [`src/local_zone.zig`](../src/local_zone.zig), [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig), [`src/zone/parser.zig`](../src/zone/parser.zig), [`src/zone/signer.zig`](../src/zone/signer.zig), [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig), [`src/cli/query.zig`](../src/cli/query.zig), and [`src/main.zig`](../src/main.zig) | Lowercasing construction is asserted in [`src/dns/name.zig`](../src/dns/name.zig) ("name builds from presentation text" expects `Example.COM.` to render as `example.com.`); the received case and the canonical form side by side in [`src/dns/name.zig`](../src/dns/name.zig) ("RFC 4343 4.1: the received case is kept, the canonical form folds it"); the 0x20 label-first case rule and the case-insensitive comparison of the randomized name in [`src/dns/name.zig`](../src/dns/name.zig) ("0x20: the first letter of each label takes a random case, the rest keeps its case"); the question's case in [`src/dns/question.zig`](../src/dns/question.zig) ("RFC 4343 4.1: the question keeps the case the client sent"); the echoed response question in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) ("RFC 4343 4.1: the response echoes the question in the case the client sent"); the zone's own stored case on the wire in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) ("RFC 4343 4.1: the zone's own stored case reaches the wire", "an AXFR answer carries the owner name the zone file spelled"); the signature over the canonical form and the served name that is not in [`src/zone/signer.zig`](../src/zone/signer.zig) ("RFC 4034 6.2: the signature is over the canonical form and the served name is not"); and the digest of a mixed-case zone in [`src/zone/signer.zig`](../src/zone/signer.zig) ("RFC 8976: the ZONEMD digest of a mixed-case zone is the digest of its lower-case twin"); the upstream query's case in [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig) ("RFC 4343 4.1: the upstream query keeps the case the client sent"); the NSEC3 hash over the canonical name in [`src/dns/dnssec.zig`](../src/dns/dnssec.zig) ("RFC 5155 5: the NSEC3 hash covers the canonical owner name, not the case it arrived in"); the trust-anchor coverage of an upper-case name in [`src/dns/validate.zig`](../src/dns/validate.zig) ("RFC 1035 2.3.2: a trust anchor covers a name that arrived in upper case"); the local-zone match in [`src/local_zone.zig`](../src/local_zone.zig) ("RFC 1035 2.3.2: the local zone table matches a name regardless of case"); name and question round-trip coverage in [`src/dns/name.zig`](../src/dns/name.zig) and [`src/dns/message.zig`](../src/dns/message.zig); the question name on the wire with and without the randomization, and the printed echoed question, in [`src/cli/query.zig`](../src/cli/query.zig) ("the query packet carries the question name as typed", "0x20: the question name on the wire varies only in the case of the label-first letters", and "the question the server echoed is printed with the case it carries"); the CLI's own randomized query in [`src/main.zig`](../src/main.zig) ("the 0x20 query asks a randomized name and prints the question the server echoed"); the bit-for-bit echo of the randomized question on the wire in [`test/integration/case_randomization_test.zig`](../test/integration/case_randomization_test.zig) ("the server echoes the 0x20 question name bit for bit"); verbatim-QNAME upstream forwarding in [`test/integration/pipeline_test.zig`](../test/integration/pipeline_test.zig) ("forwarder sends full QNAME upstream"); and the wire round trip in the same file ("UDP pipeline echoes the question in the case the client sent"). |
| EDNS(0) TCP keepalive | [RFC 7828 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7828#section-3.1): the `edns-tcp-keepalive` option (code 11 per [§6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7828#section-6)), its OPTION-LENGTH of 0 when the TIMEOUT is omitted and 2 when it is present, and its TIMEOUT as the idle timeout of the TCP connection in units of 100 milliseconds; [§3.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7828#section-3.2.1): "Clients MUST specify an OPTION-LENGTH of 0 and omit the TIMEOUT value"; [§3.3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7828#section-3.3.1): "A DNS server that receives a query using UDP transport that includes the edns-tcp-keepalive option MUST ignore the option"; [§3.3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7828#section-3.3.2): a server that received the query on a TCP session may answer it with the option, and "Servers MUST specify the TIMEOUT value that is currently associated with the TCP session"; [RFC 9210 §4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9210#section-4.2): "DNS servers and clients SHOULD signal their timeout values using the edns-tcp-keepalive EDNS(0) option". | `supported` for the declared profile: option code 11 is interpreted. A query that arrives on a TCP session and carries the option is answered with it, carrying the idle timeout this server applies to that session (the listener's five-second per-frame deadline, 50 units of 100 milliseconds); a UDP query's option is ignored (RFC 7828 section 3.3.1); a query whose option carries a payload is answered FORMERR like any other malformed EDNS option; and the option is never sent unsolicited (RFC 7828 section 3.3.2 answers it only when the query carried it). The option rides the authoritative zone answer, so a forwarded answer and a locally synthesized answer (RFC 6303, RFC 6761, RFC 7793, RFC 8375) carry none. | `supported` for the same subset: the option is answered over TCP with the server's own idle timeout, the UDP path never carries it, and a malformed option is answered FORMERR. What stays out: the RFC 9210 section 4.2 limit on the total duration of a TCP connection, which is a different thing; the configurable idle timeout of RFC 9210 section 4.5, which the fixed five-second deadline is not; and the `edns-tcp-ack-keepalive` acknowledgement, which no RFC defines (RFC 7828 has no such option and no unicast-response bit). | [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig) (the option code), [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) (the answer, the framing, and the malformed-option rule), [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig) (`Runtime.answerFromTcp`, the entry point that names the transport the answer is built for), and [`src/platform/listener.zig`](../src/platform/listener.zig) (the TCP frame loop, which dispatches every frame through that entry point) | The option's framing and the server's idle timeout in the answer, the UDP silence, the FORMERR for a malformed option, and the "not asked for" case in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) ("RFC 7828 section 3.3.2: a TCP query with the keepalive option is answered with the server's idle timeout", "RFC 7828 section 3.3.1: the keepalive option is answered only on a TCP session", "RFC 7828 section 3.3.2: a query without the keepalive option is answered with none", and "RFC 7828 section 3.2.1: a malformed keepalive query is answered FORMERR"), and the transport split on the request path in [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig) ("RFC 7828: a TCP query with the keepalive option is answered with it, the same query over UDP is not"), and the option reaching a real client in [`src/platform/listener.zig`](../src/platform/listener.zig) ("RFC 7828: a TCP client is answered with the keepalive option"), where a loopback client sends the option on a TCP session and reads the idle timeout back out of the answer. |
| DNS name server identifier (NSID, option code 3) | [RFC 5001 §2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5001#section-2.1)–[§2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5001#section-2.2): a resolver signals with an empty NSID option and MUST NOT send payload data; a server that chooses to honor it answers with its own identifier and MUST ignore any payload in the query. [§2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5001#section-2.3): option code 3 with an opaque OPTION-DATA. [§2.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5001#section-2.4) and [§3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5001#section-3.1): the payload's content is the operator's choice, presented as two hexadecimal digits per octet. [§2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5001#section-2.2): the option MUST NOT be sent to a resolver that did not request it. | `supported`: an NSID option is parsed with RFC 6891 framing and never rejected, a duplicate NSID is not an error, query payload data is never read, and the authoritative path answers a request with the operator's configured payload and nothing else: a repeated request is answered once, with one option, not refused. The bundled CLI asks for the identifier with `--nsid` and renders the payload it receives as hexadecimal per RFC 5001 section 2.4, two digits per octet, so the round trip is exercised end to end. **There is no default identifier**: nothing is derived from an address, a name, or a hostname, because RFC 5001 section 3.1 warns that an address- or name-derived ID defeats the purpose of the option. Without an `nsid` directive the server holds no identifier and answers none. | `supported`: the option code, request-only answer, ignored query payload, and operator-supplied payload are implemented, with loopback socket evidence; the bundled CLI asks for the identifier with `--nsid` and renders the received payload as hexadecimal per RFC 5001 section 2.4, so it is the reference client for the round trip. | [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig), [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig), [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig), [`src/config/parser.zig`](../src/config/parser.zig), [`src/config/loader.zig`](../src/config/loader.zig), [`src/cli/query.zig`](../src/cli/query.zig), [`src/main.zig`](../src/main.zig) | Option framing, duplicate, and multi-instance parse tests in [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig); RFC 5001 request-only, configured-versus-unconfigured, ignored-payload, and repeated-request answer tests in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig); the CLI's RFC 5001 section 2.4 hexadecimal rendering in [`src/cli/query.zig`](../src/cli/query.zig) ("query formatter prints the NSID payload as hexadecimal per RFC 5001 section 2.4"); directive accept/reject tests in [`src/config/parser.zig`](../src/config/parser.zig); hex-to-OCTET decoding into the runtime policy in [`test/config/config_test.zig`](../test/config/config_test.zig). Wire evidence: IPv4 loopback UDP/TCP tests asserting the configured payload bytes on the response OPT (`NSID with configured payload` in [`test/integration/pipeline_test.zig`](../test/integration/pipeline_test.zig)), and the CLI round trip end to end (`the query CLI asks for NSID and prints the server's identifier` in the same file), which asserts the CLI's own empty RFC 5001 request, the server's answer, and the hexadecimal rendering. |
| Trust anchor key tags (edns-key-tag, option code 14) | [RFC 8145 §4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8145#section-4.1): option code 14 carrying one or more 16-bit Key Tags, with OPTION-LENGTH equal to twice their count, and §4.2.2.1's separate instances of the option. [§4.2.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8145#section-4.2.2.1): a validating resolver SHOULD include the option in its own DNSKEY queries; [§4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8145#section-4.2): a client MUST NOT include it for a non-DNSKEY query. [§4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8145#section-4.3): a responder MUST NOT include the option in any DNS response. | `supported`: on the forwarding path a validating resolver — one with trust anchors configured through `trust-anchor-file` — adds the option to its own upstream DNSKEY query for a zone an anchor speaks for, using a DS's Key Tag field (RFC 4034 section 5.1.4) or a DNSKEY's RFC 4034 Appendix B checksum, and never adds it to a non-DNSKEY query or to a query for an unanchored name. The authoritative path never answers with it, satisfying §4.3 by construction: only requested NSID and ZONEVERSION options are ever appended. An incoming edns-key-tag is parsed with RFC 6891 framing and **not** validated against §4.1's length rule, because no MUST in RFC 8145 requires rejecting one and dropping a query the RFC never asks to drop would be a regression against the RFC 6891 unknown-option rule. The §5.2 Key Tag query form is implemented for the same validating resolver: whenever it originates a DNSKEY chain query for a zone an anchor names exactly, it also originates the §5.1 QTYPE NULL query for `_ta-<sorted four-digit hex tags>.<zone>` over the same channel (iterate or configured forwarder), best effort and never for a non-anchor zone or from the chain cache. The §8 logging of collected Key Tags is outside this profile and is named as an exclusion, not as a gap in the subset above. | `supported`: the querier-side SHOULD is met for this server's single forwarding mode, with loopback socket evidence; the responder MUST NOT, the non-DNSKEY prohibition, and the OPTION-LENGTH definition are honored. Excluded from the profile: the §8 logging of collected Key Tags and incoming-option length validation beyond RFC 6891 framing. The §5.2 Key Tag query form is sent by `fetchZoneRecords` on the DNSKEY chain path for an exact anchor zone, bounded to the twelve tags a 63-octet label holds, and the new resolver unit tests pin the name it builds (sorting, zero padding, exact-owner filter, tag cap, overlong-name refusal) while the new loopback test pins the upstream query it emits. | [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig), [`src/dns/validate.zig`](../src/dns/validate.zig), [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig), [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) | Per-anchor key-tag, DNSKEY-checksum, non-DNSKEY, and unanchored-name tests in [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig); multi-instance and single-tag parse tests in [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig); the responder MUST NOT test in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig). Wire evidence: loopback forwarder test capturing the upstream DNSKEY query and asserting the anchor key-tag bytes (`forwarder signals anchor key tags on upstream DNSKEY query` in [`test/integration/pipeline_test.zig`](../test/integration/pipeline_test.zig)). |
| Zone version (ZONEVERSION, option code 19) | [RFC 9660 §2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9660#section-2.1): option code 19 with OPTION-LENGTH zero in a query and, in a response, a 1-octet LABELCOUNT, a 1-octet TYPE, and the opaque VERSION. [§3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9660#section-3.1) and [§3.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9660#section-3.2.2): one empty option per query, and the option is hop-by-hop and not transitive. [§3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9660#section-3.2): which responses carry it — including referrals, NXDOMAIN, NODATA, and SERVFAIL — and [§3.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9660#section-3.2.1): FORMERR when OPTION-LENGTH is not zero or the option appears twice. [§4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9660#section-4): TYPE 0 (SOA-SERIAL) carries a copy of the zone's SOA SERIAL, and [§6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9660#section-6.1) assigns code 19. | `supported`: a well-formed request is answered on the authoritative path with LABELCOUNT equal to the responding zone's label count (0 for the root), TYPE 0, and that zone's own SOA serial — the referring zone's for a referral, which is what §3.2 requires. A malformed request is answered FORMERR through the existing malformed-response builder rather than being dropped or ignored, the option is never emitted unsolicited or forwarded, and `zoneversion off` is the operator declining §3.2's optional answer. The option is not interpreted on the forwarding path, which is what §3.1's "SHOULD NOT send the ZONEVERSION option to non-authoritative name servers" asks for. TYPE 0 is the only type RFC 9660 defines, so no defined type is missing; the bundled `query` CLI now asks for the option with `--zoneversion` and renders its OPT pseudosection (`; ZONEVERSION: SOA-SERIAL <serial> (<zone>.)`, RFC 9660 §2.2 and §4.1), so the full §3.1 round trip is exercised end to end and the CLI is the reference client for it. | `supported`: the wire behavior for the one defined TYPE, the FORMERR rule, and the not-transitive rule are implemented, with loopback socket evidence; the bundled CLI asks with `--zoneversion` and renders the OPT pseudosection, so it is the reference client for the round trip. | [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig), [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig), [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig), [`src/zone/index.zig`](../src/zone/index.zig), [`src/config/parser.zig`](../src/config/parser.zig), [`src/config/loader.zig`](../src/config/loader.zig), [`src/cli/query.zig`](../src/cli/query.zig), [`src/main.zig`](../src/main.zig) | SOA-SERIAL answer, referral, NXDOMAIN, unsolicited-suppression, `zoneversion off`, and FORMERR tests in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig); response-framing parse tests in [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig); directive accept/reject tests in [`src/config/parser.zig`](../src/config/parser.zig); runtime plumbing in [`test/config/config_test.zig`](../test/config/config_test.zig); CLI OPT pseudosection rendering in [`src/cli/query.zig`](../src/cli/query.zig) ("query formatter prints the OPT pseudosection with the RFC 9660 zone version"). Wire evidence: IPv4 loopback UDP/TCP tests asserting the LABELCOUNT/TYPE-0/serial option bytes on the response OPT (`ZONEVERSION with SOA serial` in [`test/integration/pipeline_test.zig`](../test/integration/pipeline_test.zig)). |
| DNS Error Reporting (RFC 9567) | [RFC 9567 §4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9567#section-4): the unsolicited EDNS0 Report-Channel option that carries the agent domain, and the rule that it is never present when the configured agent domain is empty or is the null label. [§5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9567#section-5): option code 18, with the agent domain as a fully qualified domain name in uncompressed DNS wire format. [§6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9567#section-6.1): the reporting resolver builds the report query, sends it as a TXT query, keeps the option out of the query, bounds the reports with a depth limit, and prefers TCP. [§6.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9567#section-6.1.1): the report QNAME is the `_er` label, the QTYPE, the labels of the name that failed, the extended DNS error code, the `_er` label again, and the agent domain. [§6.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9567#section-6.2): at most one Report-Channel option in a response. [§6.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9567#section-6.3): the monitoring agent answers a report positively with a TXT record, and sets the truncation bit for a UDP query that carries no DNS Cookie. [§8.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9567#section-8.1): the agent domain must not be a subdomain of the domain the server reports on. [§8.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9567#section-8.2): the monitoring agent must not answer NXDOMAIN for a domain it monitors. | `supported`: all three roles of the RFC are implemented and reachable through the configuration. The authoritative server advertises its agent domain in an unsolicited EDNS(0) Report-Channel option (option code 18) in the OPT record of its answers, with the agent domain in uncompressed wire form; the option appears at most once, never when the configured agent domain is empty or the root (§4), never when the query carried no EDNS(0) (RFC 6891 §§6.1.1 and 7), and never for a zone the agent domain is a subdomain of (§8.1, compared on label boundaries, so `xfoo.example.` is not a subdomain of `foo.example.`). The reporting resolver reads the agent domain and the error code out of the upstream's answer before the forwarding path strips the upstream's options, builds the §6.1.1 report QNAME from the client's QTYPE, the client's QNAME, the error code, and the agent domain, refuses a name over 255 octets, and sends it as a TXT query that carries no Report-Channel option of its own; a report is sent only for a client query, never for the answer to a report query, so reports cannot cascade (§6.1). The report travels the server's own forward path, so its answer is validated and cached like any other answer, which is the dampening §4 relies on. The monitoring agent answers a report-shaped TXT query under the monitored domain positively, with one TXT record at the queried name, and never answers a name at or below that domain with NXDOMAIN (§8.2), which would make a resolver treat the domain as unreachable and stop the reports. The deviations are named rather than hidden: the report travels through the configured upstream, because this server is a forwarder with no recursion of its own and the upstream resolves the report name; the report prefers the upstream TCP path and falls back to the ordinary path on a platform without it, which is the §6.1 SHOULD in its degraded form; the report is sent before the client's answer is written, so the error path pays one extra round trip to the same upstream; the monitored domain must sit inside a zone this server serves, and belongs in an unsigned zone, because the synthesized TXT record is not in the zone and carries no RRSIG, which §8.2 itself gives as the solution; the advertisement rides on the answers of a zone the server serves, so a refusal and a referral carry none; the report's QTYPE label is one decimal QTYPE, the multi-QTYPE form being a Work in Progress document; and a report is sent when this server's own validation of the upstream's answer failed, which is the §4.1 example, or when the upstream's answer carried an EDE with a non-zero INFO-CODE. | `supported`: the Report-Channel option, the report generator, and the monitoring agent are all implemented and operator-reachable, with the deviations the baseline column names as the shape of this build rather than as missing work. Excluded from the profile: the multi-QTYPE form of the report QNAME, which the RFC's own source marks as a Work in Progress, and the resolution of the report name itself, which this server does not perform because it has no recursion of its own. | [`src/dns/error_report.zig`](../src/dns/error_report.zig), [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig), [`src/config/parser.zig`](../src/config/parser.zig), [`src/config/loader.zig`](../src/config/loader.zig), [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig), [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig), [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig) | The report-name and agent-domain tests in [`src/dns/error_report.zig`](../src/dns/error_report.zig): the §4.1 vector `_er.1.broken.test.7._er.a01.agent-domain.example.`, the refusal of a report name over 255 octets, the agent domain's round trip through the option payload, the refusal of an empty, a trailing-byte, and a compression-pointer payload, and the report-name recognition for the right and for the wrong agent. The advertisement tests in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig): exactly one option whose payload is the agent domain's wire form, no option without EDNS(0), no option without an agent, none for the root, none for a zone the agent domain is a subdomain of, and none on a refusal or a referral. The monitoring-agent tests in the same file: the positive TXT answer at the queried name, the NODATA answer that stands where an NXDOMAIN would be, the truncation bit over UDP with no DNS Cookie, and its absence over TCP and on a query that carries a cookie. The report-generation tests in [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig): the exact report name the upstream saw, the absence of a Report-Channel option in the report query, no report without an option, with an empty payload, with a malformed payload, or with a report name over 255 octets, and no second report from the answer to a report query. The directive tests in [`test/config/error_report_agent_test.zig`](../test/config/error_report_agent_test.zig) and [`test/config/error_report_monitor_test.zig`](../test/config/error_report_monitor_test.zig). Wire evidence: IPv4 loopback UDP tests in [`test/integration/error_report_test.zig`](../test/integration/error_report_test.zig): the Report-Channel option code and its agent-domain payload on a real answer's OPT record, a query without EDNS(0) answered with no OPT record at all, the monitoring agent's TXT answer to a report query with its TTL, its text, and the truncation bit, and a monitored name answered with no data and the zone's SOA instead of a name error. |
| Oversized responses and large RRs over TCP | [RFC 7766 §8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7766#section-8) and [RFC 6891 §4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6891#section-4.3) "UDP Message Size": datagram limits with truncation and TCP retry, and the 65535-octet TCP message ceiling. | `supported`: TCP accepts a full 65535-octet frame and both transports share one `Runtime.answer` path, so no separate large-RR path is required; TC truncation is applied only by datagram callers and EDNS payload negotiation is floored at 512 and capped at 1232. Integration test `oversized answer is truncated over UDP and served whole over TCP` (pipeline_test.zig) serves a 100-record RRset whose wire form exceeds 1232 octets: whole over TCP, TC=1 with zero answers over UDP. RFC 8078 is CDS/CDNSKEY trust management rather than a large-RR transport document, and this profile implements neither CDS nor CDNSKEY. | `supported`: the same shared answer path plus the oversized-response UDP/TCP transport test referenced in the evidence column. | [`src/platform/listener.zig`](../src/platform/listener.zig), [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig), [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig) | Frame-ceiling and truncated-frame coverage in [`src/platform/listener.zig`](../src/platform/listener.zig) ("TCP framing accepts the largest u16 length-prefixed frame", "TCP loop rejects truncated frames before serving the next connection"); TC and EDNS payload negotiation in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig); and large opaque DNSSEC RDATA preservation in [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig). Integration test `oversized answer is truncated over UDP and served whole over TCP` sends and receives a 100-record answer over 1232 octets (TCP case); UDP case confirms truncation to <= 1232 octets with TC=1. |
| Header bit semantics (CD, AD, reserved bits) | [RFC 1035 §4.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc1035#section-4.1.1): the CD and AD header bits, and the Z field's "Reserved for future use. Must be zero in all queries and responses"; [RFC 4035 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4035#section-3), [§3.1.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4035#section-3.1.6), [§3.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4035#section-3.2.2), [§3.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4035#section-3.2.3), and [§4.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4035#section-4.6): a non-validating server must not set AD, the AD bit of a query must be ignored (section 3: "a security-aware name server MUST ignore the setting of the AD bit in queries"), the CD bit of the query is copied into the response, and CD handling on the recursive side. | `supported`: the response header flags are decided in one place, `ResponseFlags.header` in [`src/dns/header.zig`](../src/dns/header.zig), which the answer builder in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) uses for the authoritative, empty, refused, not-implemented, FORMERR, and bad-version answers: QR is set, OPCODE and RD are copied from the query, CD is copied from the query (RFC 4035 section 3.2.2), and AD is the responder's own claim about the answer, never a bit taken from the query. The authoritative path never sets AD, while the forwarding path sets it only on validated-secure answers (`src/resolver.zig`) and clears any upstream AD first. The reserved Z bit is modelled instead of dropped at parse time: `Header.parse` records the received 0x0040 bit in `Header.reserved`, and `Header.encode` never writes it, so RFC 1035 section 4.1.1's "must be zero in all queries and responses" holds on every path, including the composed upstream query. 0x0040 is the only unassigned bit left in the RFC 1035 Z field; AD (0x0020) and CD (0x0010) are assigned by RFC 4035, and 0x0004 is RCODE bit 2, not a reserved bit. CD is still copied into the authoritative answer, which RFC 4035 section 3.1.6 only recommends clearing (a SHOULD, not a requirement); the copy is deliberate, because the authoritative path never validates and the client's "I validate myself" intent stays the same on the authoritative, forwarding, and transfer answers. The forwarder relays CD into cached, relayed, and error responses, and CD=1 suppresses aggressive-NSEC synthesis and DNSSEC-presence EDE without stripping RRSIGs. An inbound AD bit is ignored by the authoritative path and cleared before the forwarder composes its upstream query (RFC 4035 section 4.6). An inbound bit that the RFCs require to be zero is counted instead of silently dropped: the answer path in [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig) bumps `zimdns_reserved_bit_queries_total` for a query that arrives with the reserved Z bit set and `zimdns_ad_bit_queries_total` for a query that arrives with the AD bit set, and both counters are printed by `formatPrometheus` in [`src/metrics.zig`](../src/metrics.zig), so an operator reads them in the Prometheus output of `zimdns server run --print-metrics`. | `supported`: the explicit header policy is in place — QR set, OPCODE and RD copied, CD copied, AD only on a validated-secure forwarded answer, and the reserved Z bit always zero on the wire and never copied from a request. The last named gap is closed: inbound-bit logging is implemented, with `zimdns_reserved_bit_queries_total` and `zimdns_ad_bit_queries_total` in [`src/metrics.zig`](../src/metrics.zig), incremented in the answer path in [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig) and printed in the Prometheus output. The row is `supported` for its named subset because every part it names is implemented and proven: the single-source header policy, the authoritative path that never sets AD, the forwarder that sets AD only on validated-secure answers, the reserved bit that is never emitted, and the two inbound-bit counters. The one deliberate deviation stays stated in the baseline: CD is copied into the authoritative answer, where RFC 4035 section 3.1.6 only recommends clearing it. | [`src/dns/header.zig`](../src/dns/header.zig), [`src/dns/message.zig`](../src/dns/message.zig), [`src/metrics.zig`](../src/metrics.zig), [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig), [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig), [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig) | The reserved-bit rule is unit-tested in [`src/dns/header.zig`](../src/dns/header.zig) ("the reserved Z bit is parsed but never encoded", "the response flag policy copies CD and clears the reserved bit") and end to end in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) ("a query with the reserved bit set is answered with the reserved bit clear", "the empty, refused, and FORMERR answers follow the same header flag policy"); the AD rule is unit-tested in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) ("a query with the AD bit set is answered with AD clear", and "an authoritative answer to a signed and valid zone never sets AD") and asserted in [`test/golden/golden_test.zig`](../test/golden/golden_test.zig); the CD rule is unit-tested in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) ("the CD bit is copied from the query into the authoritative response") and into AXFR/NOTIFY transfer answers in [`src/zone/transfer.zig`](../src/zone/transfer.zig) ("transfer AXFR answers echo the query CD bit", NOTIFY mirror test); CD and DO cache isolation, aggressive-NSEC CD handling, and CD-preserving error responses are unit-tested in [`src/resolver.zig`](../src/resolver.zig), and the forwarder clearing the client's AD bit before composing its upstream query is unit-tested in the same file ("RFC 4035 section 4.6: buildUpstreamQuery clears the client's AD bit"). The inbound QR-bit gate is tested in [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig) ("authoritative runtime drops response packets instead of answering them"). The inbound-bit counters are unit-tested in [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig) ("authoritative runtime records metrics for successful, bad, and bit-flagged queries"): a query that arrives with the reserved Z bit set bumps the reserved-bit counter through the network answer path, a query that arrives with the AD bit set bumps the AD counter through the stream answer path, a query that carries neither leaves both counters at zero, and the Prometheus text of the same counters carries both names with the counted values; the metric names and their values are asserted again in [`src/metrics.zig`](../src/metrics.zig) ("metrics format emits prometheus counters"). |
| Zone digest (ZONEMD, type 63) | [RFC 8976 §2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8976#section-2.2): type 63 RDATA with the 32-bit Serial, the 8-bit Scheme, the 8-bit Hash Algorithm, and the untruncated Digest field; [§2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8976#section-2.3) presentation format; [§3.3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8976#section-3.3.1) SIMPLE collation over the [RFC 4034 §6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4034#section-6.1)–[§6.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4034#section-6.3) canonical RR form and order, with RRsets of one owner additionally ordered by ascending numeric RR TYPE; [§3.3.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8976#section-3.3.1.1) inclusion and exclusion rules; [§3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8976#section-3.1) placeholder record; [§3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8976#section-3.2) optional signing step; [§3.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8976#section-3.4) digest-writing step; [§4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8976#section-4) verification; [§5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8976#section-5.2) and [§5.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8976#section-5.3) registries (SIMPLE = 1; SHA384 = 1, SHA512 = 2). |`partial`: SIMPLE (1) with SHA384 and SHA512 is computed and verified, and the server refuses to start on a zone whose apex ZONEMD does not match, so the capability is operator-reachable. The §3.3.1.1 rules are implemented for everything this profile can load: all zone data including glue and occluded RRs is included, exact owner/class/type/RDATA duplicates collapse to one, records outside the zone are excluded, the apex ZONEMD RRset is excluded, every other DNSSEC RR in the zone file is included, the RRSIG covering the apex ZONEMD RRset is excluded, and the records the server itself generates when it signs a zone at startup are excluded because no publisher could have covered them. RFC 8976 §4 steps 1 to 3 are implemented for a zone that is named by a configured trust anchor: step 1 decides from the local anchors whether signatures are expected (an anchor for the zone says yes, an anchor for a zone above it does not, because a delegation without a DS is insecure and the parent's side of that chain is not reachable from a zone file), and a zone that only a parent or a root anchor names is excluded from the §4 chain: the anchor does not name the zone, so no signature is expected for it and the step 2 existence check is skipped for it, step 2's existence requirement is met by the validated signature over the ZONEMD RRset when the zone carries one and by the zone's own denial records when it carries none (the NSEC at the apex, or the NSEC3 whose owner hash matches the apex, must deny the ZONEMD type and carry a signature that chains to the anchor), and step 3 requires the SOA and ZONEMD RRsets to carry valid signatures chaining up to that anchor, directly for a DNSKEY anchor and through the zone's own DNSKEY RRset and the signature that RRset carries for a DS anchor. The chain is checked before the digest, so a zone that is not authentic is refused at startup with its own error, never on the strength of its digest alone. A proven absence resolves as "the zone publishes no ZONEMD", which is a different answer from a failed verification: a zone that publishes a ZONEMD that does not match the anchor is refused, and a zone that publishes none is accepted, so the denial proof is what tells the two apart. An unproven absence — a zone that holds no NSEC or NSEC3 record to deny the RRset, or a zone that states two sets of NSEC3 parameters, where the apex NSEC3PARAM alone governs the chain (RFC 5155 §7.1) — is refused at startup with its own error, because absent and unknown cannot be told apart. That refusal is this profile's own fail-closed rule and not RFC 8976 §4's requirement: step 2 asks only that a proven absence means digest verification cannot occur, and it says nothing about an absence that no proof settles. A zone no trust anchor names keeps today's behavior: the digest is checked as a checksum only. The catalog consumer runs that digest check on the catalog and on every member it fetches at startup and on a `NOTIFY`-driven refresh; the anchor-based authenticity steps are startup-only because the runtime holds no trust anchors. Not implemented: step 1's parent-side DS lookup (a DS the parent publishes for the zone, which needs the parent zone); scheme 240-254 and hash algorithm 240-254 (Private Use) are rejected as unverifiable rather than computed. The operator-reachable half is verification: a zone whose apex ZONEMD does not match, or whose chain does not validate, is refused at startup. Digest computation exists in [`src/zone/zonemd.zig`](../src/zone/zonemd.zig) and is exercised by the verifier's tests, but there is no operator-facing publish command, because the CLI is exactly two commands; that is a named gap of this row. The §3.2 signing step belongs to the startup signer: a zone signed with `dnssec-key-file` gets one RRSIG over its
ZONEMD RRset, made after the digest exists, pinned by the loader test "a signed zone carries an RRSIG over its ZONEMD RRset". Such a zone is digested over
its zone-file records, because the records the signer generates are excluded from the entry set, so its served digest
matches the file's data rather than the RFC's digest of the fully signed zone; that is a deviation this verifier and
this server share, and one no foreign publisher's digest hits, because everything a foreign signed zone carries is in
its file. An RFC 2136 update reseals the digest: the update's canonical zone text is rewritten with a fresh digest for every apex ZONEMD record and the new SOA serial before the file is persisted and the signed zone installed (pinned by "an update reseals a published ZONEMD" in [`src/zone/update.zig`](../src/zone/update.zig)), so a zone that takes dynamic updates restarts cleanly. Digest computation accepts SIMPLE with SHA384 and SHA512, the only scheme and hashes RFC 8976 defines,
and the deployment practice of §3.4 (the ZONEMD RR and the matching SOA published together, the SOA serial unchanged by
signing) stays with the operator.|Same as the baseline, and the §4 chain is part of it: verification is a load-time gate that checks the trust-anchor chain before the digest, and digest generation exists in [`src/zone/zonemd.zig`](../src/zone/zonemd.zig) without an operator-facing command. The §3.2 signing step, scheme and hash algorithms beyond SHA384 and SHA512, and an operator-facing way to publish the record stay out of the first release: the signing and the deployment stay with the operator. The parent-side DS lookup stays out of the first release: it needs the parent's DS, and this profile reads no parent zone. A zone that only a parent or a root anchor names is excluded from the §4 chain, so the step 2 existence check is skipped for it. The startup refusal of an absence that no proof settles is this profile's own fail-closed rule and not the RFC's requirement. The §4 step 2 denial branch is in: the zone's own NSEC and NSEC3 records are what this profile already reads.|Typed RDATA in [`src/dns/rdata.zig`](../src/dns/rdata.zig) (`Zonemd`, wire code 63 in [`src/dns/types.zig`](../src/dns/types.zig)) with a 12-octet digest floor and a 64-octet ceiling; zone-text parsing in [`src/zone/parser.zig`](../src/zone/parser.zig); canonical ordering and digest computation in [`src/zone/zonemd.zig`](../src/zone/zonemd.zig) (the computation and the verifier share one `computeDigest`); the trust-anchor chain over the apex RRsets in [`src/dns/validate.zig`](../src/dns/validate.zig) (`Anchors.anchorsZone` and `zoneSignedToAnchor`, reusing the RFC 4035 validator and its canonical signed-data assembly); the startup gate in [`src/config/loader.zig`](../src/config/loader.zig) (`ZonemdSignatureInvalid` for a broken chain, `ZonemdMismatch` for a broken digest, `ZonemdAbsenceUnproven` for an absence no record of the zone proves); the §4 step 2 denial branch in [`src/dns/validate.zig`](../src/dns/validate.zig) (`zoneZonemdAbsence`, which reads the zone's own NSEC and NSEC3 records and reuses the RFC 4035 and RFC 5155 denial checks the validator already applies to a response).|The three RFC 8976 Appendix A example zones are asserted byte-exact: A.1 (SHA384), A.2 (uppercase owners, a wildcard, an occluded RR, an exact duplicate, a non-apex ZONEMD, an out-of-order MX and AAAA RRset, and one out-of-zone RR), and A.3 (SHA384, its SHA512 digest verified on a zone carrying only that record, and its two Private Use scheme and hash records rejected as unverifiable). The RFC 4034 §6.1 canonical-order worked example is checked pairwise against the comparator. A differential test proves the §3.3.1.1 DNSSEC rule in both directions: adding an apex RRSIG covering the ZONEMD leaves the digest unchanged, while removing an unrelated apex RRSIG changes it. A mismatch test asserts the operator report carries the RFC's own digest as the computed value. The §4 chain is proven on both sides: a zone signed with the RFC 8080 §6.1 Ed25519 key chains to a DNSKEY anchor holding that key and to a DS anchor holding that key's published DS, the same signatures written in a zone file in the RFC 4034 §3.2 presentation form chain as well, the same zone does not chain to an anchor holding the RFC 6605 §6.1 P-256 key, does not chain when it carries no signature, and needs no chain when no anchor names the zone. Loading is proven end to end by `ServerContext.load`: a zone whose apex ZONEMD is signed to the configured trust anchor loads (and its RFC 8976 A.1 digest still matches after the server signs the zone, because the records the server generates are outside the digest), the same zone signed to another key is refused with the distinct `ZonemdSignatureInvalid` error, a zone whose digest does not match is still refused with `ZonemdMismatch`, and a zone whose digest matches while the configured anchor names another zone still loads on the digest alone. The §4 step 2 denial branch is proven on both sides: a signed zone that publishes no ZONEMD is accepted because the NSEC at the apex denies the type, and the same zone published with an NSEC3 chain is accepted because the NSEC3 whose owner hash matches the apex denies it, while a zone that holds no such proof leaves the absence unknown and is refused at startup with the distinct `ZonemdAbsenceUnproven` error, and a zone that carries a second apex NSEC3PARAM leaves the absence unknown the same way, because the parameters of the chain come from the apex NSEC3PARAM (RFC 5155 section 7.1: a zone with a second apex NSEC3PARAM leaves the absence unproven), and a signed zone whose ZONEMD digest does not match is still refused with `ZonemdMismatch`.|
| Question count for OPCODE 0 (RFC 9619) | [RFC 9619 §4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9619#section-4), which updates RFC 1035: "A DNS message with OPCODE = 0 MUST NOT include a QDCOUNT parameter whose value is greater than 1."; "A DNS message with OPCODE = 0 and QDCOUNT > 1 MUST be treated as an incorrectly formatted message. The value of the RCODE parameter in the response message MUST be set to 1 (FORMERR)."; and "Such firewalls MUST NOT treat messages with OPCODE = 0 and QDCOUNT = 0 as malformed." The RFC is a Standards Track update whose subject is OPCODE 0 only; [Appendix A.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9619#appendix-A.1) is informative and is not a conformance claim here. | `supported` for OPCODE 0: a received OPCODE 0 message with QDCOUNT > 1 is rejected at parse time and answered RCODE 1 (FORMERR) with the first question echoed, while QDCOUNT = 0 is declined with no response at all, so it is never answered FORMERR. A QDCOUNT > 1 message with any other OPCODE is answered FORMERR (`Message.parse` reports TooManyQuestions uniformly). Excluded from the profile: the AXFR/IXFR/NOTIFY transfer profiles keep their own section layouts, so Appendix A's QDCOUNT 0 transfer messages are not a claim of this row, and the informative Appendix A.1 is not a conformance claim. | `supported`: the OPCODE 0 subset — QDCOUNT > 1 answered FORMERR with the first question echoed, QDCOUNT = 0 never answered FORMERR — with unit and socket evidence; the transfer profiles stay with the Zone transfers row. | [`src/dns/message.zig`](../src/dns/message.zig) (`Error.TooManyQuestions` and the header-only parse used to echo the question), [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig) (the FORMERR decision for both the unsigned and the TSIG-stripped parse), [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) (`buildFormerrResponse`) | The QDCOUNT > 1 error value is asserted in [`src/dns/message.zig`](../src/dns/message.zig) ("message accepts unassigned opcode and rejects unsupported question count" and "message rejects a well-formed two-question message before parsing sections", which pins that the question count itself is the format error and that the header-only parse still yields the first question for the error answer), and the wire behaviour end to end in [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig) ("RFC 9619: an OPCODE 0 query with QDCOUNT 2 is answered FORMERR" checks QR, RCODE 1, QDCOUNT 1, an empty answer section, and the echoed question), ("RFC 9619: a TSIG-signed OPCODE 0 query with QDCOUNT 2 is answered FORMERR", which repeats the same check for the signed path, where the TSIG record is stripped before the query is parsed), and ("RFC 9619: an OPCODE 0 query with QDCOUNT 0 is never answered FORMERR", which pins the MUST NOT against over-applying FORMERR). A QDCOUNT > 1 message carrying another OPCODE is answered FORMERR as well ("RFC 9619: a non-query OPCODE with QDCOUNT 2 is answered FORMERR"). |
| Minimal responses to QTYPE=ANY (RFC 8482) | [RFC 8482 §4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8482#section-4.2): "If there is no CNAME present at the owner name matching the QNAME, the resource record returned in the response MAY instead be synthesized. In this case, a single HINFO resource record SHOULD be returned. The CPU field of the HINFO RDATA SHOULD be set to "RFC8482". The OS field of the HINFO RDATA SHOULD be set to the null string", plus the operator-chosen TTL and the §4.2 signing rule: "If the DNS query includes DO=1 and the QNAME corresponds to a zone that is known by the responder to be signed, a valid RRSIG for the RRsets in the answer (or authority if answer is empty) section MUST be returned. In the case of DO=0, the RRSIG SHOULD be omitted"; [§4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8482#section-4.1) single-RRset and subset mode: "A DNS responder that receives an ANY query MAY decline to provide a conventional ANY response or MAY instead send a response with a single RRset (or a larger subset of available RRsets) in the answer section", "Where multiple RRsets exist, the responder SHOULD choose a small subset of those available to reduce the amplification potential of the response", and "If the zone is signed, appropriate RRSIG records MUST be included in the answer"; [§4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8482#section-4.3) the type list the subset draws from: "Some implementations have implemented the spirit of this document by returning all RRsets of RRTYPE CNAME, MX, A, and AAAA that are present at the owner name while suppressing others"; and [§7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8482#section-7): "the TC bit SHOULD NOT be set in the response". The signature over the answer is an [RFC 4034 §3.1.8.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4034#section-3.1.8.1) signature over the [§6.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4034#section-6.2) canonical RR form, carrying the [§3.1.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4034#section-3.1.3) Labels field, the [§3.1.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4034#section-3.1.4) original TTL, the [§3.1.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4034#section-3.1.6) key tag, and the [Appendix B](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4034#appendix-B) key-tag calculation. [§4.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8482#section-4.4): "A DNS responder MAY behave differently when processing ANY queries received over different transports, e.g., by providing a conventional ANY response over TCP whilst using one of the other mechanisms specified in this document in the case where a query was received using UDP" and "Implementers MAY provide configuration options to allow operators to specify different behavior over different transports"; [§9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8482#section-9): "Queries with QTYPE=ANY are frequently observed as part of reflection attacks, since a relatively small query can be used to elicit a large response" and "The optional behavior described in this document to reduce the size of responses to queries with QTYPE=ANY is compatible with the use of DNSSEC by both initiator and responder". RFC 8482 updates RFC 1034 and RFC 1035. | `supported`: the authoritative path answers an OPCODE 0, class IN, QTYPE=ANY query at an owner that exists and carries no CNAME with a synthesized HINFO RRset (CPU `RFC8482`, empty OS) plus, when the owner holds more than one RRset, every RRset of the types RFC 8482 section 4.3 names — CNAME, MX, A, and AAAA — in the order the RFC lists them; every other type at the owner (TXT, DNSKEY, SRV, and the rest) is suppressed. An owner that holds a single RRset is answered with the HINFO alone, an owner whose section 4.3 set is empty is answered with the HINFO alone, an owner that carries a CNAME is answered with that single CNAME RRset per section 4.1, an empty non-terminal keeps NODATA, and a nonexistent name keeps NXDOMAIN. Every RRset the answer carries is signed when the zone is signed and the query sets DO=1: the HINFO's RRSIG is made at serve time over the RFC 4034 section 6.2 canonical form, the zone's own RRSIG rides along over each RRset that joined it, and the synthesized HINFO carries only the signature the server made for it, because a signature over an RRset the answer does not hold never rides along (RFC 4035 section 5.3.1); the whole answer validates secure with the project's own validator. A zone that carries its own DNSSEC records and no `dnssec-key-file` is answered with its own RRsets and no synthesized HINFO, because the server has no key to sign one with and section 4.1 requires the RRSIGs of every RRset the answer holds. The answer carries no TC bit (section 7): an RRset that does not fit the payload the client advertised is dropped and the RRsets that fit are kept. The HINFO keeps the operator's TTL (`minimal-any-ttl`, `minimal-any-ttl-tcp`) and the RRsets that join it keep the zone's own TTL. The section 4.4 conventional ANY response over TCP is a MAY the server declines: it answers the same way over UDP and TCP and varies only the HINFO's TTL. The forwarding path still relays QTYPE=ANY upstream unchanged. | `supported`: the section 4.2 single-HINFO mode, the section 4.1 CNAME case, the section 4.1 subset as the section 4.3 type list — the HINFO plus every CNAME, MX, A, and AAAA RRset at the owner — and the section 4.2 DO=1 RRSIG over the synthesized HINFO; the section 4.4 conventional ANY response over TCP is a MAY the server declines, it answers the same way over both transports, and a zone that carries its own DNSSEC records is answered with its own RRsets and no synthesized HINFO. | the null OS string, which the synthesized RRset now carries as the typed HINFO of the Typed HINFO RDATA row — an empty authority section, TC clear both on the built message and after a wire round-trip, the CNAME owner answering with the CNAME RRset, the empty non-terminal staying NODATA, and the missing name staying NXDOMAIN Subset evidence: "RFC 8482 section 4.1: an ANY query at an owner with more than one RRset answers a subset" in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) asserts that an owner holding an A, an AAAA, and a TXT is answered with the HINFO and the A RRset alone — the A because §4.3's list puts it ahead of the AAAA, the TXT because §4.3 does not name it — with TC clear and an empty authority section; "RFC 8482 section 4.1: the RRset that joins the HINFO keeps the zone's own TTL" asserts that the HINFO takes the operator's TTL while the RRset that joined it keeps the zone's own, over UDP and over TCP alike; "RFC 8482 section 4.1: a DO=1 ANY query on a signed zone signs every RRset of the subset" asserts that the answer carries an RRSIG over the HINFO and an RRSIG over the RRset that joined it, each with the type it covers, and validates the whole answer secure with the project's own validator in [`src/dns/validate.zig`](../src/dns/validate.zig); "RFC 8482: the answers the subset does not reach keep their bytes" asserts the wire bytes of the single-RRset, CNAME, empty-non-terminal, and nonexistent-name answers, which are the bytes the server sent before the subset existed. Configured-TTL evidence: `buildResponseWithPolicy` with `.minimal_any_ttl = 300` serves that value and the unconfigured policy keeps 86400 (`src/auth/response.zig`, "RFC 8482: a configured TTL reaches the synthesized HINFO"). Transport evidence: "RFC 8482: the minimal ANY answer takes the TTL of the transport it arrived over" in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) asserts the UDP TTL and the TCP TTL of the same ANY query, that the base TTL serves both transports when no TCP TTL is set, that the synthesized HINFO still carries CPU `RFC8482` and the null OS, and that the two answers differ in the TTL octets alone; "RFC 8482: the minimal ANY TTL follows the transport the query arrived over" in [`src/auth/runtime.zig`](../src/auth/runtime.zig) answers the same ANY query over UDP and over TCP and asserts each transport's TTL; "config parser accepts minimal-any-ttl-tcp as a guarded unsigned TTL" in [`src/config/parser.zig`](../src/config/parser.zig) and "server context carries the minimal ANY TTLs into the answer policy" in [`src/config/loader.zig`](../src/config/loader.zig) carry the operator's values into the answer policy; the DO=1 test in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) also asserts that the RRSIG over the synthesized HINFO carries the TCP TTL as its original TTL. DO=1 signature evidence: "RFC 8482: a DO=1 ANY query on a signed zone answers the HINFO with a valid RRSIG" in [`src/auth/response.zig`](../src/auth/response.zig) asserts the HINFO plus one RRSIG, the type covered, the Labels field of the owner, the original TTL, and the key tag of the zone DNSKEY, and validates the answer secure with the project's own validator in [`src/dns/validate.zig`](../src/dns/validate.zig); the same test asserts that a query without DO carries no RRSIG, and "RFC 8482: an unsigned zone answers the HINFO with no RRSIG" asserts the unsigned case. End-to-end evidence: "a signed zone served by the runtime answers a DO=1 ANY query with a valid RRSIG" in [`src/config/loader.zig`](../src/config/loader.zig) loads a config with `dnssec-key-file`, answers a DO=1 ANY query over the runtime, validates the answer secure against the DNSKEY the zone publishes, and asserts that neither the key-file secret nor its decoded octets reach the wire.
